## SR-MoA (Self-Routed MoA) Implementation Plan

### 1. Recommended Architecture: Option C (Gated Self-Routing), with ERMoE-inspired basis vectors as a second arm

**Rationale for Option C as primary, with Option A as secondary:**

After studying the full codebase, I recommend **Option C (Gated Self-Routing)** as the primary method, with **Option A (Eigenbasis Self-Routing)** as a comparison arm. Here is the reasoning:

**Why not Option B (Activation-Norm):**
Option B requires every expert to compute a partial forward pass on raw input to determine routing weights. The current 5 canonical experts (MeanPool, LastToken, MaxPool, AttentionPool, Conv1dPool) operate on *hidden states* `(B, 64, 512)`, not raw input `(B, 512)`. You would need to create a parallel raw-input partial-forward pathway for each expert, essentially doubling the expert count. This adds complexity with unclear benefit given the 5-expert scale (AoE targets 700M-4B-parameter LLMs where partial forwards are meaningful).

**Why Option C is best for this paper:**
1. It naturally extends the paper's narrative arc. The story goes: "normalization destroys routing signal -> external routers on raw input fix it (RR-MoA) -> but external routers are still fragile, so let each expert gate itself on raw input (SR-MoA)." This makes the fix feel less "trivially obvious" because the method *eliminates the router entirely*.
2. Each expert gets its own sigmoid gate `g_k = sigmoid(w_k^T * x_raw + b_k)` -- a single learned linear function of the raw input. The gate's output serves as both the expert's routing weight and its activation magnitude. This is a total of `5 * (512 + 1) = 2,565` routing parameters -- far fewer than the ~1,100 in the Conv1d router, making it more parameter-efficient.
3. It aligns with the paper's Proposition 2 about sufficient statistics. Each expert's gate learns its own projection of the raw signal, potentially specializing in different aspects (one expert might trigger on high variance, another on trend presence).
4. It completely removes the softmax bottleneck, top-K selection, and load-balancing auxiliary losses, which simplifies the method and removes hyperparameters.
5. The `run_sr_moa.py` file already exists with a spectral-routing variant (MSE=0.986, much worse than RR-MoA's 0.527). We should repurpose this name or create a new file.

**Why also include Option A (Eigenbasis):**
Option A (cosine similarity to learned basis vectors) provides a second "self-routing" variant that connects to a different literature (ERMoE), and the cosine similarity formulation has nice geometric interpretation. It is cheap to implement and adds a useful comparison point. If it works, the paper can present both as instances of a general "self-routing" principle.

### 2. Exact Implementation Plan

**File**: Create `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py` (new name to avoid collision with existing `run_sr_moa.py` which is the spectral-routed variant).

**Class: `SelfRoutedMoA`**

```python
class SelfRoutedMoA(nn.Module):
    """Self-Routed Mixture of Adapters.
    
    Eliminates external router entirely. Each expert has an internal gate
    that determines its activation from raw input -- experts route themselves.
    
    Three self-routing modes:
      - 'gated': g_k = sigmoid(Linear(x_raw))  per expert (Option C)
      - 'eigenbasis': score_k = cos_sim(proj(x_raw), basis_k)  (Option A) 
      - 'hybrid': g_k = sigmoid(cos_sim(proj(x_raw), basis_k) / tau)
    """
```

**Key parameters and their values:**

| Parameter | Value | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| `d_model` | 512 (MOMENT-small) | From backbone |
| `output_dim` | 96 (forecast horizon) | Standard |
| `input_len` | 512 | Raw time series length |
| `K` | 5 | Same expert pool for fair comparison |
| `hidden` | 64 | Same as RR-MoA experts |
| `routing_mode` | "gated" / "eigenbasis" / "hybrid" | Self-routing variant |
| `gate_proj_dim` | 64 | Projected dimension for gating (reduces from 512) |
| `temperature` | 1.0 | For eigenbasis softmax scaling |

**Forward pass (Gated mode -- primary):**

```
raw_input: (B, 512)
hidden_states: (B, 64, 512)  -- from frozen MOMENT

# Per-expert gating on raw input
For each expert k:
    z_k = self.gate_proj_k(raw_input)  # (B, 64) via Linear(512, 64)
    g_k = sigmoid(self.gate_score_k(z_k))  # (B, 1) via Linear(64, 1)
    o_k = expert_k(hidden_states)  # (B, output_dim)

output = sum(g_k * o_k for k in range(K)) / sum(g_k)  # Renormalized
```

Alternatively, the simplest formulation (and the one I recommend starting with):

```
# Minimal variant: single projection shared across experts
z = self.shared_proj(raw_input)  # (B, 64) via Linear(512, 64) + GELU
For each expert k:
    g_k = sigmoid(self.gate_k(z))  # (B, 1) via Linear(64, 1)
    o_k = expert_k(hidden_states)  # (B, output_dim)

output = sum(g_k * o_k) / (sum(g_k) + eps)
```

**Parameter count estimate (gated mode):**
- Shared projection: `512 * 64 + 64 = 32,832`
- Per-expert gates: `5 * (64 + 1) = 325`
- Per-expert adapter heads: same as RR-MoA, approximately `(512*64 + 64 + 64*96 + 96) * 5 = ~197,600` for the 5 MLP-style heads (varies by expert type)
- **Total router params: ~33,157** (vs RR-MoA's ~1,100 for Conv1d router + 320 for router_head = ~1,420)
- **Total model params: ~230K** -- well within A10G VRAM

Wait -- actually the minimal variant should be even simpler. Looking at the RR-MoA router, it is `Conv1d(1,16,32,16) + GELU + AdaptiveAvgPool1d(4) + Linear(64,5)` = ~1,100 params. For the self-routed variant, each expert just needs a single learned vector that computes a scalar score from raw input. The most elegant version:

**Minimal Gated Self-Routing (recommended for implementation):**

```python
# Per-expert gate: a single linear layer from raw input to scalar
self.gates = nn.ModuleList([
    nn.Sequential(
        nn.Linear(input_len, 1),  # 512 + 1 = 513 params per expert
    )
    for _ in range(K)
])
# Total routing params: 5 * 513 = 2,565

# Forward:
gate_scores = [sigmoid(self.gates[k](raw_input)) for k in range(K)]  # each (B, 1)
expert_outs = [self.adapters[k](hidden_states) for k in range(K)]     # each (B, H)
weights = torch.stack(gate_scores, dim=1)  # (B, K, 1)
weights = weights / (weights.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)  # renormalize
outputs = torch.stack(expert_outs, dim=1)  # (B, K, H)
return (weights * outputs).sum(dim=1)
```

But this is too many params per gate (512 per expert). Better with a projection:

```python
# Shared feature extraction from raw input (like RR-MoA's Conv1d but simpler)
self.raw_proj = nn.Sequential(
    nn.Linear(input_len, 64),  # 512*64 + 64 = 32,832 params
    nn.GELU(),
)
# Per-expert scoring head
self.gate_heads = nn.Linear(64, K)  # 64*5 + 5 = 325 params
# Total routing: ~33,157 params

# Forward:
z = self.raw_proj(raw_input)  # (B, 64)
gate_logits = self.gate_heads(z)  # (B, K)
gates = torch.sigmoid(gate_logits)  # (B, K) -- each in [0, 1]
# No softmax, no top-K -- gates are independent
# Renormalize for weighted combination:
weights = gates / (gates.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)  # (B, K)
```

Actually, there is a critical design decision here. There are two distinct formulations:

**Formulation 1 (Shared-proj + per-expert score):** This is essentially RR-MoA with sigmoid instead of softmax. The shared projection is still an external router that happens to use sigmoid gates. This does NOT satisfy "experts route themselves."

**Formulation 2 (Each expert has its own gate):** Each expert contains its own gating sub-network that operates on raw input. There is NO shared routing infrastructure. This is the true self-routing formulation.

For paper impact, **Formulation 2 is essential** -- it must be architecturally clear that each expert independently decides its own activation. Here is the refined design:

**Class structure:**

```python
class SelfRoutedExpert(nn.Module):
    """An expert with an internal self-routing gate."""
    def __init__(self, base_expert, input_len, gate_hidden=16):
        super().__init__()
        self.expert = base_expert  # e.g., MeanPoolHead(512, 96, 64)
        # Internal gate: raw_input -> scalar activation
        self.gate = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_len, gate_hidden),  # 512*16 + 16 = 8,208
            nn.GELU(),
            nn.Linear(gate_hidden, 1),          # 16 + 1 = 17
        )  # Total: ~8,225 per expert
    
    def forward(self, hidden_states, raw_input):
        g = torch.sigmoid(self.gate(raw_input))  # (B, 1)
        out = self.expert(hidden_states)          # (B, output_dim)
        return g, out

class SelfRoutedMoA(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, input_len=512, K=5, 
                 hidden=64, gate_hidden=16, renorm=True):
        super().__init__()
        self.K = K
        self.renorm = renorm
        self.experts = nn.ModuleList([
            SelfRoutedExpert(
                HEAD_CLASSES[i % len(HEAD_CLASSES)](d_model, output_dim, hidden),
                input_len, gate_hidden
            )
            for i in range(K)
        ])
    
    def forward(self, hidden_states, raw_input):
        gates, outputs = [], []
        for expert in self.experts:
            g, o = expert(hidden_states, raw_input)
            gates.append(g)    # (B, 1)
            outputs.append(o)  # (B, output_dim)
        
        gates = torch.cat(gates, dim=1)       # (B, K)
        outputs = torch.stack(outputs, dim=1)  # (B, K, output_dim)
        
        if self.renorm:
            weights = gates / (gates.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)
        else:
            weights = gates / self.K  # Simple average weighting
        
        return (weights.unsqueeze(-1) * outputs).sum(dim=1)
```

**Parameter count (gate_hidden=16):**
- Per-expert gate: `512*16 + 16 + 16*1 + 1 = 8,225`
- 5 experts: `5 * 8,225 = 41,125` routing params
- Expert heads: ~85,000 (same as RR-MoA)
- Total: ~**126K** (vs RR-MoA's ~426K -- significantly smaller)

But actually, `gate_hidden=16` with 5 experts gives `41K` routing params which is more than RR-MoA's router (~1.4K). For fair comparison, we should also test **minimal gates** with `gate_hidden=1` (pure linear gate) = `513 * 5 = 2,565` total routing params.

**Eigenbasis variant (Option A):**

```python
class EigenbasisSelfRoutedMoA(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, input_len=512, K=5, 
                 hidden=64, basis_dim=64, temperature=0.1):
        super().__init__()
        self.K = K
        self.temperature = temperature
        self.input_proj = nn.Linear(input_len, basis_dim)  # shared projection
        # Each expert has a learned basis vector
        self.basis_vectors = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(K, basis_dim))
        nn.init.orthogonal_(self.basis_vectors)  # start orthonormal
        self.adapters = nn.ModuleList([...])  # same 5 experts
    
    def forward(self, hidden_states, raw_input):
        z = self.input_proj(raw_input)  # (B, basis_dim)
        z_norm = F.normalize(z, dim=-1)
        b_norm = F.normalize(self.basis_vectors, dim=-1)  # (K, basis_dim)
        scores = z_norm @ b_norm.T  # (B, K) cosine similarities
        weights = F.softmax(scores / self.temperature, dim=-1)
        outputs = torch.stack([a(hidden_states) for a in self.adapters], dim=1)
        return (weights.unsqueeze(-1) * outputs).sum(dim=1)
```

**Parameter count (eigenbasis, basis_dim=64):**
- Input projection: `512*64 + 64 = 32,832`
- Basis vectors: `5*64 = 320`
- Total routing: ~33,152 params

### 3. Experiment Grid

**Budget: ~2-4 hours of GPU time on a single A10G.**

Given MOMENT-small runtime of ~45s per run on ETTh1 (from CLAUDE.md), and the fact that SR-MoA adds minimal overhead to RR-MoA (same expert heads, just different routing), I estimate ~50s per run.

**Core experiments (Priority 1 -- must have):**

| Experiment | Configs | Datasets | Seeds | Runs | Est. time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Gated SR-MoA (gate_hidden=16) | 1 | ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather | 42,43,44 | 9 | ~8 min |
| Gated SR-MoA (gate_hidden=1, minimal) | 1 | ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather | 42,43,44 | 9 | ~8 min |
| Eigenbasis SR-MoA (basis_dim=64) | 1 | ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather | 42,43,44 | 9 | ~8 min |
| RR-MoA baseline (rerun for fair comparison) | 1 | ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather | 42,43,44 | 9 | ~8 min |

**Priority 1 subtotal: 36 runs, ~32 min**

**Ablations (Priority 2 -- important for paper):**

| Experiment | Purpose | Runs | Est. time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gated SR-MoA with renorm vs no-renorm | Does renormalization matter? | 9 | ~8 min |
| Gated SR-MoA with sigmoid init bias = {0, -2, +2} | Gate initialization sensitivity | 18 | ~15 min |
| Extend best SR-MoA to all 6 datasets | Full dataset coverage | 18 | ~20 min |
| SR-MoA routing entropy/diversity diagnostics | Paper diagnostics | 0 (computed from results) | 0 |

**Priority 2 subtotal: 45 runs, ~43 min**

**Extended evaluation (Priority 3 -- if time allows):**

| Experiment | Runs | Est. time |
|---|---|---|
| 5-seed extension on core 3 datasets | 15 | ~13 min |
| Hybrid mode (sigmoid of cosine similarity) | 9 | ~8 min |
| AdaMix self-routed (hidden-state gates, collapse control) | 9 | ~8 min |

**Priority 3 subtotal: 33 runs, ~29 min**

**Total: 114 runs, ~104 min = ~1.75 hours** -- well within budget.

### 4. How This Strengthens the Paper

**Narrative positioning:** SR-MoA should be presented as a **natural theoretical consequence** of the diagnosis, NOT as a replacement for RR-MoA. The narrative arc becomes:

1. **Diagnosis** (unchanged): normalization destroys MI needed for routing.
2. **Minimal fix** (RR-MoA): bypass normalization for the router. This is the core contribution.
3. **Principled extension** (SR-MoA): if external routers are fragile (and all 11 rescue mechanisms fail), then eliminate the router entirely -- let experts self-select via learned alignment with raw input.
4. **Theoretical justification**: SR-MoA operationalizes the paper's own Proposition 2 at the expert level -- each expert's gate learns its own sufficient statistic from the raw input, so the MI destruction by normalization is bypassed per-expert rather than per-router.

**Where it fits in the paper:**
- Add a **subsection in Section 4** (Experiments) after the main RR-MoA results, titled something like "From External to Self-Routing: SR-MoA"
- Add a row to `tab:baselines` for SR-MoA
- If SR-MoA beats RR-MoA, promote it to a co-main-result
- If SR-MoA matches RR-MoA, present it as "confirming the diagnosis -- multiple router-free architectures all work once you read raw input, because the routing signal is genuinely present in the raw data"

**Key reviewer-defense argument:** "Our diagnosis predicts that *any* mechanism reading raw input should avoid collapse. SR-MoA confirms this: even without a centralized router, expert self-routing on raw input achieves competitive/better MSE, because the information-theoretic bottleneck is in the normalization, not in the router architecture. This validates the diagnosis and rules out the alternative hypothesis that RR-MoA's gains are an artifact of a specific router design."

### 5. Comparison Baselines

The results table should include:

| Method | Router | Routing input | #Route params |
|---|---|---|---|
| AdaMix | External (hidden-state) | Post-norm hidden states | ~1.4K |
| AdaMix (no RevIN) | External (hidden-state) | Pre-norm hidden states | ~1.4K |
| RR-MoA (conv) | External (Conv1d+pool) | Raw time series | ~1.4K |
| RR-MoA (ssr) | External (Linear) | [mu, sigma] | ~10 |
| **SR-MoA (gated)** | **Self-routing** | **Raw time series** | **2.6K -- 41K** |
| **SR-MoA (eigenbasis)** | **Self-routing** | **Raw time series (projected)** | **33K** |
| Ind. Ensemble | None (uniform 1/K) | N/A | 0 |
| RR-MoA (uniform) | None (fixed 1/K) | N/A | 0 |

Key comparisons:
- **SR-MoA vs RR-MoA**: Does self-routing match/beat external routing?
- **SR-MoA vs Ind. Ensemble**: Does learned self-routing beat uniform mixing?
- **SR-MoA vs AdaMix (no RevIN)**: Self-routing on raw vs external routing on pre-norm hidden states?
- **SR-MoA routing entropy**: Is it naturally diverse (no load-balancing loss needed)?

### 6. Risk Assessment

**Risk 1: SR-MoA does not beat RR-MoA.**
- **Mitigation**: This is still a useful result. Frame as: "multiple routing architectures work equally well on raw input, confirming the bottleneck is in the normalization, not the router design."
- **Probability**: ~40%. RR-MoA's Conv1d router is quite effective at extracting temporal features; a simpler gate may underperform on datasets where temporal structure matters for routing.

**Risk 2: SR-MoA gates collapse (all experts get similar gate values).**
- **Mitigation**: This would actually be interesting -- it would mean "on raw input, all experts are equally competent, so there's nothing to route." Monitor gate value distributions across test set.
- **Probability**: ~20%. The sigmoid gates can saturate at 0 or 1, creating a different kind of collapse. Gate initialization bias of -2 (from Residual-IA+ experience) may help.

**Risk 3: Eigenbasis vectors collapse to the same direction.**
- **Mitigation**: Initialize with orthogonal vectors; add a diversity penalty `||B^T B - I||_F` if needed.
- **Probability**: ~30%. Without explicit orthogonality constraint, 5 basis vectors in 64-dim space may converge.

**Risk 4: SR-MoA adds too many parameters and the comparison is unfair.**
- **Mitigation**: Use the minimal gate variant (gate_hidden=1, 2,565 total routing params) which is actually comparable to RR-MoA's ~1,400 routing params.
- **Probability**: Low. The parameter difference is small relative to the expert heads (~85K).

**Risk 5: Implementation takes too long (>2 days).**
- **Mitigation**: The implementation is straightforward -- it is structurally simpler than RR-MoA (no Conv1d router, no top-K selection, no load-balancing loss). The codebase already has all infrastructure (data loading, backbone, expert heads, training loop, evaluation, JSON saving). Estimated implementation: 4-6 hours.
- **Probability**: Very low.

**Fallback plan**: If SR-MoA fails to produce interesting results, the paper can still use the existing run_sr_moa.py (spectral routing) results as an ablation showing that "even within raw-input routing, the choice of features matters -- time-domain features outperform spectral features." This is already supported by the existing data (spectral SR-MoA MSE=0.986 vs RR-MoA MSE=0.527).

### 7. Implementation Sequence

**Day 1 (coding, ~6 hours):**
1. Create `scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py` with `SelfRoutedExpert` and `SelfRoutedMoA` classes
2. Implement three routing modes: `gated`, `eigenbasis`, `hybrid`
3. Reuse existing training loop pattern from `run_rr_moa.py` (same optimizer, scheduler, evaluation)
4. Add `--routing-mode` CLI argument and all standard flags
5. Add routing diagnostics (per-expert gate mean/std, entropy analog, cross-sample variance)
6. Smoke test locally (CPU, 1 epoch, verify no crash)
7. rsync to RACE VM

**Day 2 (experiments, ~4 hours GPU):**
1. Run Priority 1 grid (36 runs, ~32 min)
2. Analyze Priority 1 results, decide which variant to extend
3. Run Priority 2 grid (45 runs, ~43 min)  
4. Run Priority 3 if time allows
5. rsync results back

**Day 3 (writing, ~4 hours):**
1. Analyze all results, compute significance
2. Add SR-MoA subsection to main.tex
3. Add rows to comparison tables
4. Update verify.py with new claims
5. Update abstract/intro if SR-MoA produces compelling headline numbers

- `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py` -- primary reference for class structure, training loop, CLI, JSON output format, expert heads (import HEAD_CLASSES, HEAD_NAMES)

Here's the summary of what the research surfaced and what I'm proposing:

**Literature findings**: Three strong 2025-2026 papers (ERMoE, AoE, Routing-Free MoE) all converge on the same idea — eliminate external routers entirely and let experts self-determine their activation. This directly extends our diagnosis: if normalization kills routers, the principled fix is to remove the router, not just patch its input.

**Proposed method: Self-Routed MoA (SR-MoA)** — two variants:
1. **Gated**: each expert has its own sigmoid gate on raw input (no router, no softmax, no top-K)
2. **Eigenbasis**: cosine similarity between projected raw input and expert-specific learned basis vectors

**GPU plan**: ~114 runs, ~2 hours on the A10G. Priority 1 (core comparison) finishes in ~32 min.

**Paper value**: Whether SR-MoA wins, ties, or loses vs RR-MoA, it validates the diagnosis — the MI bottleneck is in normalization, not router architecture.

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Self-Routed Mixture of Adapters (SR-MoA) — Literature-Inspired Enhancement

## Context

The RR-MoA paper (NeurIPS 2026, ~58-68% acceptance) diagnoses normalization-induced routing collapse and fixes it by routing on raw input. The main vulnerability: reviewers may dismiss the fix as "trivially obvious." Academic research via Exa surfaced three highly relevant 2025-2026 papers that inspire a more principled method:

- **ERMoE** (arXiv:2511.10971) — eigenbasis-scored routing via cosine similarity, 0% collapse
- **AoE** (arXiv:2501.13074, ICML 2025) — experts self-select via internal activation norms
- **Routing-Free MoE** (arXiv:2604.00801) — each expert determines its own activation, no router

**Key insight**: If normalization kills external routers, the principled fix is to eliminate the external router entirely — let each expert self-determine its activation from raw input.

## Deliverables

### 1. New script: `scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py`

Two self-routing modes:

**Mode A — Gated Self-Routing (primary):**
Each expert wraps its adapter head with an internal sigmoid gate on raw input:
```
g_k = sigmoid(Linear_k(raw_input))    # (B, 1) per expert
o_k = expert_k(hidden_states)          # (B, H) per expert
output = Σ(g_k * o_k) / Σ(g_k)        # renormalized weighted sum
```
- No external router, no softmax, no top-K, no load-balancing loss
- Gate is a 2-layer MLP: Linear(512→gate_hidden) + GELU + Linear(gate_hidden→1) per expert
- `gate_hidden=16` default, also test `gate_hidden=1` (pure linear gate, ~513 params/expert)

**Mode B — Eigenbasis Self-Routing:**
Each expert has a learned basis vector; routing = cosine similarity:
```
z = normalize(Linear(raw_input))       # (B, basis_dim) shared projection
b_k = normalize(basis_k)              # (basis_dim,) per expert
score_k = z · b_k                     # cosine similarity
weights = softmax(scores / tau)        # temperature-scaled softmax
```
- Basis vectors initialized orthogonally (diversity by construction)
- Optional diversity penalty: ||B^T B - I||_F

**Expert heads**: Same 5 canonical heads as RR-MoA (MeanPool, LastToken, MaxPool, AttentionPool, Conv1dPool) for fair comparison.

### 2. New orchestrator: `scripts/run_self_routed_race.sh`

Standard worker-sharding pattern. Result dir: `results/self_routed_moa/`.

### 3. Experiment grid (~114 runs, ~2 hours GPU)

**Priority 1 — Core (36 runs, ~32 min):**
- Gated SR-MoA (gate_hidden=16): ETTh1/ETTm1/Weather × seeds {42,43,44} × H=96
- Gated SR-MoA (gate_hidden=1): same grid
- Eigenbasis SR-MoA (basis_dim=64): same grid
- RR-MoA rerun for timestamp-matched comparison: same grid

**Priority 2 — Ablations (45 runs, ~43 min):**
- Gate init bias {0, -2}: 18 runs
- Extend best variant to all 6 datasets: 18 runs
- Renorm vs no-renorm: 9 runs

**Priority 3 — Extended (33 runs, ~29 min):**
- 5-seed extension on core trio: 15 runs
- Hybrid mode (sigmoid of cosine): 9 runs
- SR-MoA on hidden states (collapse control): 9 runs

## Implementation details

### Files to create
- `scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py` — main experiment script
- `scripts/run_self_routed_race.sh` — RACE VM orchestrator

### Files to modify (later, after results)
- `main.tex` — add SR-MoA subsection + table rows
- `evidence_vm/verify.py` — add SR-MoA claim checks

### Key reuse from existing code
- Expert heads: `HEAD_CLASSES`, `HEAD_NAMES` from `run_rr_moa.py:86-87`
- Data loading: `load_standard_data()` from `run_standard_evolution.py`
- Backbone: `load_backbone()`, `_get_encoder_blocks()`, `_get_hidden_dim()` from `feasibility/model.py`
- Feature extraction: `_extract_features_batch()` from `feasibility/finetune.py`
- Training loop pattern: directly follow `train_rr_moa()` from `run_rr_moa.py:352-476`
- JSON output format: same schema as RR-MoA results

### Class structure
```python
class SelfRoutedExpert(nn.Module):
    # Wraps a base expert head with an internal raw-input gate
    __init__(base_expert, input_len, gate_hidden=16)
    forward(hidden_states, raw_input) -> (gate_value, expert_output)

class SelfRoutedMoA(nn.Module):
    # K self-routed experts, no external router
    __init__(d_model, output_dim, input_len, K, hidden, gate_hidden,
             routing_mode='gated', basis_dim=64, temperature=0.1)
    forward(hidden_states, raw_input) -> prediction
    get_routing_stats(raw_input) -> dict with gate values, entropy analog
```

### Diagnostics to log (in result JSON)
- Per-expert mean gate value across test set
- Gate entropy analog: -Σ(w_k * log(w_k)) where w_k = normalized gate
- Cross-sample gate variance (does the gate actually vary per input?)
- Per-expert activation frequency (fraction of samples where gate > 0.5)

## Paper narrative

SR-MoA fits as a **validation of the diagnosis**, not a replacement for RR-MoA:

> "Our diagnosis predicts that any mechanism reading raw input should avoid collapse. SR-MoA confirms this: even without a centralized router, expert self-routing on raw input achieves competitive MSE, because the MI bottleneck is in the normalization, not in the router architecture."

**If SR-MoA beats RR-MoA**: promote to co-main-result, emphasize that the principled extension (eliminate the router) outperforms the minimal fix.

**If SR-MoA matches RR-MoA**: equally valuable — confirms the diagnosis. Multiple architectures work once you bypass normalization.

**If SR-MoA underperforms**: still useful as ablation showing that expert-architecture-aware routing (Conv1d router) captures structure that per-expert gates miss.

## Verification

1. Smoke test locally: `python scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py --dataset ETTh1 --epochs 1 --device cpu`
2. rsync to RACE, run Priority 1 grid
3. Compare MSE/MAE/entropy vs RR-MoA and AdaMix baselines
4. If results are promising, extend to Priority 2-3
5. Update `evidence_vm/verify.py` and `main.tex` with final numbers

```

350

352	def train_rr_moa(model, blocks, X_train, Y_train, X_test, Y_test,
353	                 device="cuda", n_epochs=15, forecast_horizon=96, batch_size=128,
354	                 backbone_type="moment", K=5, hidden=64, top_k=None,
355	                 router_input_mode="raw", test_ch=None, scaler=None,
356	                 expert_pool="canonical", entropy_reg_coef=0.0, router_temp=1.0,
357	                 router_arch="conv", expert_dropout=0.0, rdgf=False,
358	                 saib_coef=0.0, freeze_router=False, alpha=0.0):
359	    """Train RR-MoA: raw-routed mixture of adapters."""
360	    hdim = _get_hidden_dim(model)
361	    adapter = RawRoutedMoA(
362	        hdim, forecast_horizon, input_len=512, K=K, hidden=hidden, top_k=top_k,
363	        router_input_mode=router_input_mode, expert_pool=expert_pool,
364	        entropy_reg_coef=entropy_reg_coef, router_temp=router_temp,
365	        router_arch=router_arch, expert_dropout=expert_dropout,
366	        rdgf=rdgf, saib_coef=saib_coef, alpha=alpha,
367	    ).to(device)
368	
369	    # V1 ablation: freeze router at random init
370	    if freeze_router:
371	        for name, p in adapter.named_parameters():
372	            if "router" in name:
373	                p.requires_grad = False
374	
375	    trainable = list(p for p in adapter.parameters() if p.requires_grad)
376	    pids = {id(p) for p in trainable}
377	    for p in model.parameters():
378	        if p.requires_grad and id(p) not in pids:
379	            trainable.append(p)
380	            pids.add(id(p))
381	
382	    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(trainable, lr=1e-3)
383	    mse_fn = nn.MSELoss()
384	    use_amp = device == "cuda"
385	
386	    train_loader = DataLoader(TensorDataset(
387	        torch.from_numpy(X_train).float(), torch.from_numpy(Y_train).float(),
388	    ), batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)
389	
390	    for epoch in range(n_epochs):
391	        model.train(); adapter.train()
392	        for bx, by in train_loader:
393	            bx_raw = bx.to(device)  # (B, 512) raw input
394	            bx_enc = bx.to(device).unsqueeze(1)  # (B, 1, 512) for backbone
395	            by = by.to(device)
396	            mask = torch.ones(bx_enc.shape[0], bx_enc.shape[2], device=device)
397	
398	            with torch.amp.autocast('cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, enabled=use_amp):
399	                feat = _extract_features_batch(model, blocks, bx_enc, mask, backbone_type=backbone_type)
400	                if rdgf:
401	                    # RDGF: main loss uses detached H (no backbone gradient from weighted path)
402	                    pred = adapter(feat.detach(), bx_raw)
403	                    loss = mse_fn(pred, by)
404	                    # Auxiliary: uniform-weighted experts on live H (backbone gets uniform gradient)
405	                    outputs_live = torch.stack([a(feat) for a in adapter.adapters], dim=1)
406	                    aux_pred = outputs_live.mean(dim=1)
407	                    loss = loss + mse_fn(aux_pred, by)
408	                else:
409	                    pred = adapter(feat, bx_raw)
410	                    loss = mse_fn(pred, by)
411	                loss = loss + adapter.load_balance_coeff * adapter.load_balance_loss(bx_raw)
412	                if adapter.entropy_reg_coef > 0:
413	                    loss = loss + adapter.entropy_reg_coef * adapter.entropy_regularization(bx_raw)
414	                if adapter.saib_coef > 0:
415	                    loss = loss + adapter.saib_coef * adapter.saib_loss(bx_raw)
416	
417	            optimizer.zero_grad()
418	            loss.backward()
419	            optimizer.step()
420	
421	    # Evaluate
422	    model.eval(); adapter.eval()
423	    test_loader = DataLoader(TensorDataset(
424	        torch.from_numpy(X_test).float(), torch.from_numpy(Y_test).float(),
425	    ), batch_size=batch_size)
426	
427	    preds, tgts = [], []
428	    all_routing = []
429	    with torch.no_grad(), torch.amp.autocast('cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, enabled=use_amp):
430	        for bx, by in test_loader:
431	            bx_raw = bx.to(device)
432	            bx_enc = bx.to(device).unsqueeze(1)
433	            by = by.to(device)
434	            mask = torch.ones(bx_enc.shape[0], bx_enc.shape[2], device=device)
435	            feat = _extract_features_batch(model, blocks, bx_enc, mask, backbone_type=backbone_type)
436	            preds.append(adapter(feat, bx_raw).float().cpu())
437	            tgts.append(by.cpu())
438	            all_routing.append(adapter.get_routing_stats(bx_raw).cpu())
439	
440	    preds, tgts = torch.cat(preds), torch.cat(tgts)
441	    routing = torch.cat(all_routing)
442	    mse = nn.MSELoss()(preds, tgts).item()
443	    mae = nn.L1Loss()(preds, tgts).item()
444	
445	    mean_routing = routing.mean(dim=0).tolist()
446	    routing_entropy = -(routing * torch.log(routing + 1e-10)).sum(dim=-1).mean().item()
447	    routing_max = routing.max(dim=-1).values.mean().item()
448	
449	    # T1.B analysis support: per-sample routing-weight variance across the
450	    # test set. A near-zero value indicates the router is effectively
451	    # constant, in which case RR-MoA is operating as a soft ensemble rather
452	    # than a per-sample mixture of specialists.
453	    routing_np = routing.float().numpy()
454	    per_sample_std = float(np.mean(np.std(routing_np, axis=1)))
455	    cross_sample_var = float(np.mean(np.var(routing_np, axis=0)))
456	
457	    names = adapter._expert_names
458	    out = {
459	        "mse": mse, "mae": mae, "param_count": adapter.param_count(),
460	        "top_k": adapter.top_k,
461	        "router_input_mode": adapter.router_input_mode,
462	        "expert_pool": adapter.expert_pool,
463	        "routing": {names[i]: round(w, 3) for i, w in enumerate(mean_routing[:len(names)])},
464	        "routing_entropy": routing_entropy,
465	        "routing_max_weight": routing_max,
466	        "routing_per_sample_std": per_sample_std,
467	        "routing_cross_sample_var": cross_sample_var,
468	    }
469	
470	    # T1.A denormalized MSE in original (un-standardized) units.
471	    if test_ch is not None and scaler is not None:
472	        mse_d, mae_d = compute_denorm_mse(preds, tgts, test_ch, scaler)
473	        out["mse_denorm"] = mse_d
474	        out["mae_denorm"] = mae_d
475	
476	    return out
477	
478	
479	def _apply_unfreeze(blocks, unfreeze):
480	    """Selectively unfreeze encoder blocks."""
481	    n = len(blocks)
482	    if unfreeze == "frozen":
483	        return  # all frozen
484	    elif unfreeze == "last2":
485	        start = max(0, n - 2)
486	    elif unfreeze == "last4":
487	        start = max(0, n - 4)
488	    elif unfreeze == "all":
489	        start = 0
490	    else:
491	        raise ValueError("Unknown unfreeze: %s" % unfreeze)
492	    for i in range(start, n):
493	        for p in blocks[i].parameters():
494	            p.requires_grad = True
495

  "content": "\"\"\"Self-Routed Mixture of Adapters (SR-MoA).\n\nEliminates the external router entirely. Each expert has an internal gate\nthat determines its activation from raw input — experts route themselves.\n\nInspired by ERMoE (arXiv:2511.10971), AoE (arXiv:2501.13074, ICML 2025),\nand Routing-Free MoE (arXiv:2604.00801). Extends the RR-MoA diagnosis:\nif normalization kills external routers, the principled fix is to eliminate\nthe router and let each expert self-determine its activation on raw input.\n\nThree self-routing modes:\n  - gated:      per-expert sigmoid gate on raw input (primary)\n  - eigenbasis: cosine similarity to learned orthogonal basis vectors\n  - hybrid:     sigmoid of cosine similarity (combines both)\n\nUsage:\n    python scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py --dataset ETTh1 --routing-mode gated\n    python scripts/run_self_routed_moa.py --dataset ETTh1 --routing-mode eigenbasis\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport math\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\nfrom torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset\n\nfrom feasibility.model import (\n    load_backbone, _get_encoder_blocks, _get_hidden_dim,\n    _disable_gradient_checkpointing,\n)\nfrom feasibility.finetune import _extract_features_batch\nfrom scripts.run_standard_evolution import (\n    load_standard_data, _detect_backbone_type, compute_denorm_mse,\n)\n\n\n# --- Expert adapter heads (same as RR-MoA for fair comparison) ---\n\nclass MeanPoolHead(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, hidden=64):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(d_model, hidden), nn.GELU(), nn.Linear(hidden, output_dim))\n    def forward(self, h):\n        return self.net(h.mean(dim=1))\n\nclass LastTokenHead(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, hidden=64):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(d_model, hidden), nn.GELU(), nn.Linear(hidden, output_dim))\n    def forward(self, h):\n        return self.net(h[:, -1, :])\n\nclass MaxPoolHead(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, hidden=64):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(d_model, hidden), nn.GELU(), nn.Linear(hidden, output_dim))\n    def forward(self, h):\n        return self.net(h.max(dim=1).values)\n\nclass AttentionPoolHead(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, hidden=64):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.attn = nn.Linear(d_model, 1)\n        self.net = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(d_model, hidden), nn.GELU(), nn.Linear(hidden, output_dim))\n    def forward(self, h):\n        w = torch.softmax(self.attn(h), dim=1)\n        return self.net((h * w).sum(dim=1))\n\nclass Conv1dPoolHead(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, hidden=64):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.conv = nn.Conv1d(d_model, hidden, kernel_size=8, stride=4, padding=2)\n        self.out = nn.Linear(hidden, output_dim)\n    def forward(self, h):\n        x = F.gelu(self.conv(h.permute(0, 2, 1)))\n        return self.out(x.mean(dim=2))\n\nHEAD_CLASSES = [MeanPoolHead, LastTokenHead, MaxPoolHead, AttentionPoolHead, Conv1dPoolHead]\nHEAD_NAMES = [\"mean\", \"last\", \"max\", \"attention\", \"conv1d\"]\n\n\nclass SelfRoutedMoA(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Self-Routed Mixture of Adapters.\n\n    No external router. Each expert determines its own activation weight\n    from raw input via one of three mechanisms:\n      - gated:      sigmoid(MLP_k(x_raw)) per expert\n      - eigenbasis: softmax(cos_sim(proj(x_raw), basis_k) / tau)\n      - hybrid:     sigmoid(cos_sim(proj(x_raw), basis_k) / tau)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, d_model, output_dim, input_len=512, K=5, hidden=64,\n                 routing_mode=\"gated\", gate_hidden=16, basis_dim=64,\n                 temperature=0.1, gate_init_bias=0.0,\n                 routing_input=\"raw\"):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.K = K\n        self.routing_mode = routing_mode\n        self.routing_input = routing_input\n        self.temperature = temperature\n        self.d_model = d_model\n\n        # Expert heads (same canonical pool as RR-MoA)\n        self.adapters = nn.ModuleList()\n        self._expert_names = []\n        for i in range(K):\n            cls = HEAD_CLASSES[i % len(HEAD_CLASSES)]\n            self.adapters.append(cls(d_model, output_dim, hidden))\n            self._expert_names.append(HEAD_NAMES[i % len(HEAD_NAMES)])\n\n        # Self-routing components\n        if routing_mode == \"gated\":\n            # Each expert has its own gate: raw_input -> scalar activation\n            self.gates = nn.ModuleList()\n            for _ in range(K):\n                if gate_hidden > 1:\n                    gate = nn.Sequential(\n                        nn.Linear(input_len, gate_hidden),\n                        nn.GELU(),\n                        nn.Linear(gate_hidden, 1),\n                    )\n                else:\n                    gate = nn.Linear(input_len, 1)\n                self.gates.append(gate)\n            # Initialize gate bias\n            if gate_init_bias != 0.0:\n                for gate in self.gates:\n                    last = gate[-1] if isinstance(gate, nn.Sequential) else gate\n                    nn.init.constant_(last.bias, gate_init_bias)\n\n        elif routing_mode in (\"eigenbasis\", \"hybrid\"):\n            # Shared projection + per-expert learned basis vectors\n            self.input_proj = nn.Linear(input_len, basis_dim)\n            self.basis_vectors = nn.Parameter(torch.empty(K, basis_dim))\n            # Initialize orthogonally for diversity\n            if K <= basis_dim:\n                nn.init.orthogonal_(self.basis_vectors)\n            else:\n                nn.init.normal_(self.basis_vectors, std=1.0 / math.sqrt(basis_dim))\n\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(\"Unknown routing_mode: %s\" % routing_mode)\n\n    def _compute_weights(self, raw_input):\n        \"\"\"Compute per-expert activation weights from raw input.\"\"\"\n        if self.routing_mode == \"gated\":\n            gate_vals = []\n            for gate in self.gates:\n                g = torch.sigmoid(gate(raw_input))  # (B, 1)\n                gate_vals.append(g)\n            gates = torch.cat(gate_vals, dim=1)  # (B, K)\n            # Renormalize to sum to 1\n            weights = gates / (gates.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)\n            return weights, gates  # weights for mixing, gates for diagnostics\n\n        elif self.routing_mode == \"eigenbasis\":\n            z = self.input_proj(raw_input)  # (B, basis_dim)\n            z_norm = F.normalize(z, dim=-1)\n            b_norm = F.normalize(self.basis_vectors, dim=-1)  # (K, basis_dim)\n            scores = z_norm @ b_norm.T  # (B, K) cosine similarities\n            weights = F.softmax(scores / self.temperature, dim=-1)\n            return weights, scores\n\n        elif self.routing_mode == \"hybrid\":\n            z = self.input_proj(raw_input)  # (B, basis_dim)\n            z_norm = F.normalize(z, dim=-1)\n            b_norm = F.normalize(self.basis_vectors, dim=-1)\n            scores = z_norm @ b_norm.T  # (B, K) cosine similarities\n            gates = torch.sigmoid(scores / self.temperature)  # (B, K)\n            weights = gates / (gates.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)\n            return weights, gates\n\n    def forward(self, hidden_states, raw_input):\n        \"\"\"Forward pass: experts self-route on raw input.\n\n        Args:\n            hidden_states: (B, T, d_model) from backbone\n            raw_input: (B, input_len) raw time series\n        Returns:\n            prediction: (B, output_dim)\n        \"\"\"\n        # Get routing input (supports hidden-state control experiment)\n        if self.routing_input == \"hidden\":\n            routing_in = hidden_states.mean(dim=1)  # (B, d_model) — collapse control\n        else:\n            routing_in = raw_input  # (B, input_len) — default\n\n        weights, _ = self._compute_weights(routing_in)  # (B, K)\n        outputs = torch.stack([a(hidden_states) for a in self.adapters], dim=1)  # (B, K, H)\n        return (weights.unsqueeze(-1) * outputs).sum(dim=1)  # (B, H)\n\n    def get_routing_stats(self, raw_input):\n        \"\"\"Return normalized routing weights for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            weights, _ = self._compute_weights(raw_input)\n        return weights\n\n    def get_gate_diagnostics(self, raw_input):\n        \"\"\"Return raw gate values and detailed diagnostics.\"\"\"\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            weights, raw_gates = self._compute_weights(raw_input)\n        return {\n            \"weights\": weights,\n            \"raw_gates\": raw_gates,\n        }\n\n    def param_count(self):\n        return sum(p.numel() for p in self.parameters())\n\n    def routing_param_count(self):\n        \"\"\"Count parameters used for routing only (not expert heads).\"\"\"\n        if self.routing_mode == \"gated\":\n            return sum(p.numel() for gate in self.gates for p in gate.parameters())\n        else:\n            count = sum(p.numel() for p in self.input_proj.parameters())\n            count += self.basis_vectors.numel()\n            return count\n\n\ndef train_self_routed_moa(model, blocks, X_train, Y_train, X_test, Y_test,\n                          device=\"cuda\", n_epochs=15, forecast_horizon=96,\n                          batch_size=128, backbone_type=\"moment\", K=5, hidden=64,\n                          routing_mode=\"gated\", gate_hidden=16, basis_dim=64,\n                          temperature=0.1, gate_init_bias=0.0,\n                          routing_input=\"raw\",\n                          test_ch=None, scaler=None):\n    \"\"\"Train SR-MoA: self-routed mixture of adapters.\"\"\"\n    hdim = _get_hidden_dim(model)\n    input_len = X_train.shape[1]\n\n    adapter = SelfRoutedMoA(\n        hdim, forecast_horizon, input_len=input_len, K=K, hidden=hidden,\n        routing_mode=routing_mode, gate_hidden=gate_hidden, basis_dim=basis_dim,\n        temperature=temperature, gate_init_bias=gate_init_bias,\n        routing_input=routing_input,\n    ).to(device)\n\n    trainable = list(p for p in adapter.parameters() if p.requires_grad)\n    pids = {id(p) for p in trainable}\n    for p in model.parameters():\n        if p.requires_grad and id(p) not in pids:\n            trainable.append(p)\n            pids.add(id(p))\n\n    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(trainable, lr=1e-3)\n    mse_fn = nn.MSELoss()\n    use_amp = device == \"cuda\"\n\n    train_loader = DataLoader(TensorDataset(\n        torch.from_numpy(X_train).float(), torch.from_numpy(Y_train).float(),\n    ), batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)\n\n    for epoch in range(n_epochs):\n        model.train(); adapter.train()\n        for bx, by in train_loader:\n            bx_raw = bx.to(device)\n            bx_enc = bx.to(device).unsqueeze(1)\n            by = by.to(device)\n            mask = torch.ones(bx_enc.shape[0], bx_enc.shape[2], device=device)\n\n            with torch.amp.autocast('cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, enabled=use_amp):\n                feat = _extract_features_batch(model, blocks, bx_enc, mask, backbone_type=backbone_type)\n                pred = adapter(feat, bx_raw)\n                loss = mse_fn(pred, by)\n                # No auxiliary losses — self-routing needs no load balancing\n\n            optimizer.zero_grad()\n            loss.backward()\n            optimizer.step()\n\n    # Evaluate\n    model.eval(); adapter.eval()\n    test_loader = DataLoader(TensorDataset(\n        torch.from_numpy(X_test).float(), torch.from_numpy(Y_test).float(),\n    ), batch_size=batch_size)\n\n    preds, tgts = [], []\n    all_weights = []\n    all_raw_gates = []\n    with torch.no_grad(), torch.amp.autocast('cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, enabled=use_amp):\n        for bx, by in test_loader:\n            bx_raw = bx.to(device)\n            bx_enc = bx.to(device).unsqueeze(1)\n            by = by.to(device)\n            mask = torch.ones(bx_enc.shape[0], bx_enc.shape[2], device=device)\n            feat = _extract_features_batch(model, blocks, bx_enc, mask, backbone_type=backbone_type)\n            preds.append(adapter(feat, bx_raw).float().cpu())\n            tgts.append(by.cpu())\n            diag = adapter.get_gate_diagnostics(bx_raw)\n            all_weights.append(diag[\"weights\"].cpu())\n            all_raw_gates.append(diag[\"raw_gates\"].cpu())\n\n    preds, tgts = torch.cat(preds), torch.cat(tgts)\n    weights = torch.cat(all_weights)  # (N, K) normalized weights\n    raw_gates = torch.cat(all_raw_gates)  # (N, K) raw gate values\n\n    mse = nn.MSELoss()(preds, tgts).item()\n    mae = nn.L1Loss()(preds, tgts).item()\n\n    # Routing diagnostics\n    mean_weights = weights.mean(dim=0).tolist()\n    # Entropy of normalized weights (analogous to routing entropy in RR-MoA)\n    routing_entropy = -(weights * torch.log(weights + 1e-10)).sum(dim=-1).mean().item()\n    routing_max = weights.max(dim=-1).values.mean().item()\n\n    weights_np = weights.float().numpy()\n    per_sample_std = float(np.mean(np.std(weights_np, axis=1)))\n    cross_sample_var = float(np.mean(np.var(weights_np, axis=0)))\n\n    # Gate-specific diagnostics\n    raw_gates_np = raw_gates.float().numpy()\n    per_expert_gate_mean = [float(raw_gates_np[:, k].mean()) for k in range(K)]\n    per_expert_gate_std = [float(raw_gates_np[:, k].std()) for k in range(K)]\n    # Activation frequency: fraction of samples where gate > 0.5 (for gated mode)\n    if routing_mode == \"gated\":\n        activation_freq = [float((raw_gates_np[:, k] > 0.5).mean()) for k in range(K)]\n    else:\n        activation_freq = [float((weights_np[:, k] > 1.0 / K).mean()) for k in range(K)]\n\n    names = adapter._expert_names\n    out = {\n        \"mse\": mse, \"mae\": mae,\n        \"param_count\": adapter.param_count(),\n        \"routing_param_count\": adapter.routing_param_count(),\n        \"routing_mode\": routing_mode,\n        \"routing\": {names[i]: round(w, 3) for i, w in enumerate(mean_weights[:len(names)])},\n        \"routing_entropy\": routing_entropy,\n        \"routing_max_weight\": routing_max,\n        \"routing_per_sample_std\": per_sample_std,\n        \"routing_cross_sample_var\": cross_sample_var,\n        \"gate_diagnostics\": {\n            \"per_expert_gate_mean\": {names[i]: round(v, 4) for i, v in enumerate(per_expert_gate_mean)},\n            \"per_expert_gate_std\": {names[i]: round(v, 4) for i, v in enumerate(per_expert_gate_std)},\n            \"activation_frequency\": {names[i]: round(v, 3) for i, v in enumerate(activation_freq)},\n        },\n    }\n\n    if test_ch is not None and scaler is not None:\n        mse_d, mae_d = compute_denorm_mse(preds, tgts, test_ch, scaler)\n        out[\"mse_denorm\"] = mse_d\n        out[\"mae_denorm\"] = mae_d\n\n    return out\n\n\ndef _apply_unfreeze(blocks, unfreeze):\n    \"\"\"Selectively unfreeze encoder blocks.\"\"\"\n    n = len(blocks)\n    if unfreeze == \"frozen\":\n        return\n    elif unfreeze == \"last2\":\n        start = max(0, n - 2)\n    elif unfreeze == \"last4\":\n        start = max(0, n - 4)\n    elif unfreeze == \"all\":\n        start = 0\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"Unknown unfreeze: %s\" % unfreeze)\n    for i in range(start, n):\n        for p in blocks[i].parameters():\n            p.requires_grad = True\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    parser.add_argument(\"--dataset\", default=\"ETTh1\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--horizon\", type=int, default=96)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--K\", type=int, default=5)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--routing-mode\", default=\"gated\",\n                        choices=[\"gated\", \"eigenbasis\", \"hybrid\"],\n                        help=\"Self-routing mechanism: gated (per-expert sigmoid), \"\n                             \"eigenbasis (cosine to learned basis), hybrid (sigmoid of cosine)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--gate-hidden\", type=int, default=16,\n                        help=\"Hidden dim for gated mode (1 = pure linear gate)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--basis-dim\", type=int, default=64,\n                        help=\"Basis vector dimension for eigenbasis/hybrid modes\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--temperature\", type=float, default=0.1,\n                        help=\"Temperature for eigenbasis/hybrid softmax/sigmoid scaling\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--gate-init-bias\", type=float, default=0.0,\n                        help=\"Initial bias for gate output (negative = start inactive)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--routing-input\", default=\"raw\",\n                        choices=[\"raw\", \"hidden\"],\n                        help=\"What the self-routing gates read: raw (default) or hidden (collapse control)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--unfreeze\", default=\"frozen\",\n                        choices=[\"frozen\", \"last2\", \"last4\", \"all\"],\n                        help=\"Backbone unfreezing strategy\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--seed\", type=int, default=42)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--epochs\", type=int, default=15)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--backbone\", default=\"AutonLab/MOMENT-1-small\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--batch-size\", type=int, default=128)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--device\", default=\"cuda\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--results-dir\", default=\"results/self_routed_moa\")\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    os.makedirs(args.results_dir, exist_ok=True)\n    torch.manual_seed(args.seed)\n    np.random.seed(args.seed)\n\n    model = load_backbone(args.backbone, args.device)\n    _disable_gradient_checkpointing(model)\n    blocks = _get_encoder_blocks(model)\n    bb_type = _detect_backbone_type(args.backbone)\n\n    for p in model.parameters():\n        p.requires_grad = False\n    _apply_unfreeze(blocks, args.unfreeze)\n\n    backbone_trainable = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad)\n    print(\"Unfreeze=%s, backbone trainable params=%d\" % (args.unfreeze, backbone_trainable))\n\n    splits, _ = load_standard_data(args.dataset, args.horizon)\n    X_train, Y_train = splits[\"train\"]\n    X_test, Y_test = splits[\"test\"]\n    test_ch = splits.get(\"test_ch\")\n    scaler = splits.get(\"_scaler\")\n    print(\"%s H=%d: train=%d, test=%d\" % (args.dataset, args.horizon, len(X_train), len(X_test)))\n\n    print(\"\\nSR-MoA: %s K=%d mode=%s unfreeze=%s seed=%d\" % (\n        args.dataset, args.K, args.routing_mode, args.unfreeze, args.seed))\n    if args.routing_mode == \"gated\":\n        print(\"  gate_hidden=%d, gate_init_bias=%.1f\" % (args.gate_hidden, args.gate_init_bias))\n    else:\n        print(\"  basis_dim=%d, temperature=%.3f\" % (args.basis_dim, args.temperature))\n\n    start = time.time()\n    result = train_self_routed_moa(\n        model, blocks, X_train, Y_train, X_test, Y_test,\n        device=args.device, forecast_horizon=args.horizon,\n        backbone_type=bb_type, K=args.K, n_epochs=args.epochs,\n        batch_size=args.batch_size,\n        routing_mode=args.routing_mode, gate_hidden=args.gate_hidden,\n        basis_dim=args.basis_dim, temperature=args.temperature,\n        gate_init_bias=args.gate_init_bias,\n        routing_input=args.routing_input,\n        test_ch=test_ch, scaler=scaler,\n    )\n    elapsed = time.time() - start\n\n    print(\"SR-MoA: MSE=%.4f  MAE=%.4f  params=%d (routing=%d)  time=%.0fs\" % (\n        result[\"mse\"], result[\"mae\"], result[\"param_count\"],\n        result[\"routing_param_count\"], elapsed))\n    print(\"Routing: %s\" % result[\"routing\"])\n    print(\"Routing entropy: %.3f / %.3f (max)\" % (result[\"routing_entropy\"], math.log(args.K)))\n    print(\"Routing max weight: %.3f\" % result[\"routing_max_weight\"])\n    print(\"Gate diagnostics: mean=%s\" % result[\"gate_diagnostics\"][\"per_expert_gate_mean\"])\n    print(\"Gate diagnostics: freq=%s\" % result[\"gate_diagnostics\"][\"activation_frequency\"])\n\n    # Build filename\n    suffixes = [args.routing_mode]\n    if args.routing_mode == \"gated\":\n        suffixes.append(\"gh%d\" % args.gate_hidden)\n        if args.gate_init_bias != 0.0:\n            suffixes.append(\"gib%.1f\" % args.gate_init_bias)\n    else:\n        suffixes.append(\"bd%d\" % args.basis_dim)\n        suffixes.append(\"t%.2f\" % args.temperature)\n    if args.routing_input != \"raw\":\n        suffixes.append(\"ri-%s\" % args.routing_input)\n\n    # Backbone suffix\n    bb_lower = args.backbone.lower()\n    if \"moment\" in bb_lower and \"large\" in bb_lower:\n        suffixes.append(\"bb-moment-large\")\n    elif \"moirai-moe\" in bb_lower or \"moirai_moe\" in bb_lower:\n        suffixes.append(\"bb-moirai-moe\")\n    elif \"moirai\" in bb_lower:\n        suffixes.append(\"bb-moirai\")\n    elif \"chronos\" in bb_lower:\n        suffixes.append(\"bb-chronos\")\n    elif args.backbone != \"AutonLab/MOMENT-1-small\":\n        suffixes.append(\"bb-\" + args.backbone.split(\"/\")[-1].lower())\n\n    suffix = \"_\".join(suffixes)\n    path = \"%s/%s_H%d_K%d_%s_%d_%s.json\" % (\n        args.results_dir, args.dataset, args.horizon, args.K,\n        args.unfreeze, args.seed, suffix)\n\n    scaler_info = None\n    if scaler is not None:\n        scaler_info = {\n            \"scale_\": [float(x) for x in scaler.scale_],\n            \"mean_\": [float(x) for x in scaler.mean_],\n            \"n_features_in_\": int(getattr(scaler, \"n_features_in_\", len(scaler.scale_))),\n            \"mean_scale_sq\": float(np.mean(scaler.scale_ ** 2)),\n        }\n\n    save_data = {\n        \"dataset\": args.dataset, \"horizon\": args.horizon, \"seed\": args.seed,\n        \"K\": args.K, \"unfreeze\": args.unfreeze,\n        \"routing_mode\": args.routing_mode,\n        \"routing_input\": args.routing_input,\n        \"gate_hidden\": args.gate_hidden if args.routing_mode == \"gated\" else None,\n        \"basis_dim\": args.basis_dim if args.routing_mode != \"gated\" else None,\n        \"temperature\": args.temperature if args.routing_mode != \"gated\" else None,\n        \"gate_init_bias\": args.gate_init_bias,\n        \"backbone_trainable_params\": backbone_trainable,\n        \"sr_moa\": result,\n        \"elapsed\": elapsed,\n        \"scaler\": scaler_info,\n    }\n    with open(path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(save_data, f, indent=2, default=str)\n    print(\"Saved to %s\" % path)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

  "content": "---\nname: Self-routing MoE literature — inspiration for SR-MoA (2026-04-21)\ndescription: Three key papers (ERMoE, AoE, Routing-Free MoE) inspiring the Self-Routed MoA experiment. Cite in related work.\ntype: project\n---\n\n## Papers to cite (new baselines for Related Work §2)\n\n1. **ERMoE** — Cheng et al., arXiv:2511.10971 (Nov 2025, revised Mar 2026)\n   - Eigenbasis-scored routing: cos_sim(input, expert_orthonormal_basis)\n   - 0% routing collapse across all seeds without load-balancing loss\n   - Vision: ImageNet, COCO, Flickr30K, brain MRI\n\n2. **AoE (Autonomy-of-Experts)** — Lv et al., arXiv:2501.13074 (ICML 2025)\n   - Experts self-select via activation norm (no external router)\n   - Low-rank weight factorization reduces overhead\n   - 700M-4B scale language models\n\n3. **Routing-Free MoE** — Liu et al., arXiv:2604.00801 (Apr 2026)\n   - Eliminates router, softmax, top-K, and load balancing entirely\n   - Each expert has internal gating for self-activation\n   - Outperforms standard MoE, AoE, and ReMoE\n\n4. **MoLA** — Pan et al., arXiv:2505.17872 (May 2025)\n   - Mixture of LoRA for TS forecasting with step-specific adapters\n   - Directly relevant as concurrent TS+MoE work\n\n5. **MixFT** — Lee et al., arXiv:2603.02840 (Mar 2026)\n   - Bayesian data mixtures for TSFM adaptation\n   - Sub-domain-specific LoRA modules\n\n6. **MSFT** — Qiao et al., arXiv:2506.14087 (Jun 2025)\n   - Multi-Scale Fine-Tuning for encoder-based TSFMs (MOIRAI, MOMENT, UNITS)\n\n**Why:** These papers strengthen the related work section AND motivate SR-MoA as a principled extension of RR-MoA.\n**How to apply:** Cite 1-3 in MoE routing section, 4-6 in TSFM adaptation section. Position SR-MoA as applying the self-routing principle (from LLM/vision MoE) to the normalization-collapse diagnosis.\n"
}

- Routing entropy 1.52/1.61 = healthy, no collapse
- Hidden-state control (smoke test) confirmed collapse (MSE=1.156, near-uniform gates) — validates the normalization diagnosis

> AGENT

Still early (4/87). Here's the honest assessment assuming the gated SR-MoA trend (~10-15% better than RR-MoA) holds across datasets:

---

## Impact assessment: what SR-MoA adds

### Before SR-MoA: 58-68%

### Best case (SR-MoA consistently beats RR-MoA by 5-15%): **65-75%**

That's a **+7pp** lift. Here's why — and why it's not more:

**What SR-MoA directly fixes:**

1. **"Obvious fix" vulnerability (the biggest one) — substantially neutralized.** This was the #1 attack surface: "you just skip normalization for the router." SR-MoA eliminates the router *entirely*. The story becomes: "we diagnose the MI bottleneck → show external routers can't be rescued (720-run sweep) → show the principled extension is to eliminate the router altogether (SR-MoA), which actually *outperforms* the simple fix." This is no longer "obvious" — it's a research contribution with a clear intellectual arc from diagnosis to principled design.

2. **Novelty score for R4 (theory reviewer) lifts from 5-6 → 6.** The connection to ERMoE/AoE/Routing-Free-MoE literature positions the work within a broader trend (self-routing), but as the first to apply it to TSFMs and connect it to normalization-induced collapse. The theoretical justification (each expert learns its own sufficient statistic from raw input, operationalizing Proposition 2 at the expert level) adds depth.

3. **Experimental story becomes cleaner.** Instead of "RR-MoA is one fix that works," the paper now shows a *spectrum*: external routing on hidden states (collapses) → external routing on raw input (works) → self-routing on raw input (works better) → self-routing on hidden states (collapses again). This is a beautiful experimental arc that confirms the diagnosis from multiple angles.

**What SR-MoA does NOT fix:**

1. **DLinear gap still partially open.** SR-MoA is an adapter method — it doesn't change the fundamental representation ceiling of frozen MOMENT. DLinear will still beat frozen-backbone adapters on some cells. (~unchanged)

2. **Paper length/density.** Adding another method section + results table makes an already dense paper denser. Need careful integration — SR-MoA should be a subsection, not a new section.

3. **"One backbone, one method" concern.** If SR-MoA only works on MOMENT-small, a reviewer can still question generality. Cross-backbone SR-MoA experiments would help but aren't in the current sweep.

### Revised reviewer simulation

| Reviewer | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 (TSFM/PEFT) | 7 | **7-8** | SR-MoA as practical improvement + connection to adapter literature |
| R2 (MoE/routing) | 6-7 | **7** | Self-routing eliminates "obvious fix" criticism; connects to ERMoE/AoE |
| R3 (TS generalist) | 6-7 | **7** | Stronger results, cleaner narrative |
| R4 (Theory) | 5-6 | **6** | Modest lift; still wants tighter bound, but experimental arc is now compelling |

**Mean: 6.25 → 6.75.** The improvement is real but not transformative. The paper was already above the accept threshold for favorable reviewer draws; SR-MoA makes it more robust to unfavorable draws.

### Why not higher than 75%?

- NeurIPS base rate (~26%) means even strong papers face randomness in reviewer assignment
- The paper is still fundamentally a "diagnosis + fix" paper, not a new architecture or theory paper
- A hostile reviewer can still argue "the experts are simple MLPs; of course self-routing works when there's nothing complex to route"
- The DLinear gap remains an honest concession

### Bottom line

SR-MoA converts the paper's weakest point (trivially obvious fix) into a strength (principled design backed by recent MoE literature). The **probability shifts from coin-flip-plus to solidly above 50%**. If you get the favorable reviewer draw (R1+R2+R3), you're very likely in. If you get R4 as an outlier skeptic, you now have more ammunition for the rebuttal.

| Method | seed=42 | seed=43 | seed=44 | Mean | Entropy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SR-MoA gated gh=16** | **0.471** | **0.450** | **0.460** | **0.460** | 1.54 |

- Gated self-routing (gh=16) is **consistently ~10-13% better** than RR-MoA on ETTh1

- Eigenbasis has higher variance — seed=43 was worse (0.677), possibly temperature sensitivity
- Entropy is healthy across all gated runs (1.5+/1.6), no collapse
- ~65 seconds per run — full sweep should finish in about **85 more minutes**

Here's the consolidated picture:

### SR-MoA Results Summary (H=96, frozen backbone)

| Dataset | SR-MoA gated gh=16 | SR-MoA gated gh=1 | SR-MoA eigenbasis | SR-MoA hybrid | SR-MoA hidden (control) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ETTh1** | **0.460** (3-seed) | 0.472 | 0.580 | 0.564 | 1.031 |
| **ETTm1** | **0.375** (3-seed) | 0.370 | 0.477 | 0.408 | 0.923 |
| **Weather** | **0.215** (3-seed) | 0.204 | 0.274 | 0.252 | 0.509 |
| ETTh2 | 0.560 | — | — | — | — |
| ETTm2 | 0.308 | — | — | — | — |
| Electricity | 0.261 | — | — | — | — |

**Key findings:**
- **Gated self-routing dominates** across all 3 core datasets
- **Hidden-state control collapses** every time (MSE 2-3x worse, entropy near-max = uniform = no routing) — perfect validation of the diagnosis
- Eigenbasis underperforms gated (more variance, lower entropy)
- gh=1 (minimal gate) is competitive with gh=16, sometimes better (Weather)
- 5-seed results on ETTh1/ETTm1/Weather are stable (low cross-seed variance)

## Apples-to-apples comparison (H=96, 3-seed mean)

| Method | ETTh1 | ETTm1 | Weather |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SR-MoA gated gh=16 (frozen)** | **0.460** | **0.375** | **0.215** |
| SR-MoA gated gh=1 (frozen) | 0.472 | 0.370 | 0.204 |
| RR-MoA dense frozen | — | 0.397 | 0.236 |
| RR-MoA top2 frozen | 0.690 | 0.571 | 0.256 |
| RR-MoA top2 last4 | 0.749 | 0.571 | 0.256 |
| AdaMix frozen | 0.561 | 1.024 | 0.448 |
| **SR-MoA hidden (collapse control)** | **1.031** | **0.923** | **0.509** |

## Honest verdict: **Yes, add it — but as an appendix ablation, not a new main method.**

Here's why:

### What the results actually show

1. **SR-MoA gated beats RR-MoA frozen on ETTm1 and Weather** — but the margins are modest (0.375 vs 0.397 = 6% on ETTm1; 0.215 vs 0.236 = 9% on Weather). On ETTh1 there's no RR-MoA dense frozen baseline to compare against; vs top2 frozen (0.690) the gap is huge (33%) but that's comparing dense vs sparse routing.

2. **The hidden-state control is the real gold.** SR-MoA on hidden states collapses to MSE ~1.0 with entropy=1.60 (near-uniform = no routing). SR-MoA on raw input: MSE ~0.35-0.46 with entropy=1.5 (diverse routing). **Same architecture, same experts, only the routing input changes.** This is the cleanest causal proof of the normalization bottleneck in the entire paper.

3. **Eigenbasis and hybrid underperform gated.** This narrows the story — it's not "any self-routing works," it's "gated self-routing on raw input works." Less elegant than hoped, but still useful.

### How to integrate

**Don't** restructure the paper around SR-MoA. The paper's core is the diagnosis + RR-MoA fix. SR-MoA should be:

1. **One paragraph in §4 (Experiments)** — "To confirm the diagnosis is about the routing *input*, not the routing *architecture*, we also test Self-Routed MoA where each expert has its own sigmoid gate on raw input (no external router). SR-MoA achieves comparable/better MSE (Table X), and the hidden-state control collapses (entropy → log K), confirming the MI bottleneck is in normalization, not router design."

2. **One small table or row in an existing table** — 3 datasets × {SR-MoA raw, SR-MoA hidden} showing MSE + entropy. The contrast is the point.

3. **2-3 sentences in Related Work** citing ERMoE, AoE, Routing-Free MoE as concurrent work on self-routing.

4. **NOT a new section, NOT a headline result, NOT promoted to abstract.** The paper already has too much content at 37 pages. Adding a new main method 2 weeks before deadline risks destabilizing the narrative.

### What this buys you

- **The hidden-state control result is worth ~2-3pp of acceptance probability on its own.** It's the most convincing single-experiment validation of the diagnosis: same method, same experts, change only the routing input → performance completely inverts.
- The raw vs hidden contrast in SR-MoA is *independent* from the RR-MoA vs AdaMix contrast, the AdaMix-Raw experiment, and the Timer-XL opposite-trajectory proof. That's now **4 independent causal controls** all pointing at the same mechanism.
- Citing ERMoE/AoE/Routing-Free-MoE in Related Work shows awareness of the self-routing trend, positioning the paper correctly in the literature.

### Revised estimate: **60-72%** (up from 58-68%)

The +2-4pp comes primarily from the hidden-state control experiment, not from SR-MoA as a method.

### 1. RELATED WORK SECTION (§2) — MoE/Routing Citations
**Location: Lines 78-87**

This is the "MoE routing collapse" paragraph in the Related Work section. The exact location to add ERMoE, AoE, and Routing-Free MoE citations is here:

**Context (Lines 82-87):**
```latex
82	\textbf{MoE in Foundation Models.} Mixture-of-experts routing~\citep{fedus2022switch} has been applied within TSFM backbones: Moirai-MoE~\citep{liu2025moiraimoe} routes frequency bands across internal experts, and Time-MoE~\citep{shi2024timemoe} scales to billions of parameters via sparse activation. These apply MoE \emph{within} the backbone architecture; segment-wise approaches further specialize experts by temporal segments. In contrast, RR-MoA applies MoE at the \emph{adapter level} on a frozen backbone---an orthogonal and complementary design axis, which we empirically validate by evaluating RR-MoA \emph{on} Moirai-MoE (\S\ref{sec:cross_backbone}, Table~\ref{tab:moirai_moe}): RR-MoA still provides a $-37\%$ MSE improvement on a backbone that already contains internal expert routing, confirming the two design axes stack.
83	
84	\textbf{MoE routing collapse.} A large body of work addresses routing collapse via \emph{optimization-side} interventions: Switch Transformer load-balance~\citep{fedus2022switch}, ST-MoE z-loss~\citep{zoph2022stmoe}, Expert-Choice routing~\citep{zhou2022expertchoice}, and ReMoE ReLU gates~\citep{zhang2025remoe}. AdaMix~\citep{wang2022adamix} introduces adapter mixtures for NLP but routes on hidden states. All enforce diversity via optimization constraints without addressing whether the router's input contains discriminative information. We sweep eleven such mechanisms on MOMENT$+$RevIN and show \textbf{none recover MSE to within $2.7{\times}$ of RR-MoA} (Table~\ref{tab:rescue}). Our contribution is complementary: we identify when routing information is structurally absent from hidden states, and propose moving the router's input out of the normalized representation as the minimal causal fix.
```

82	\textbf{MoE in Foundation Models.} Mixture-of-experts routing~\citep{fedus2022switch} has been applied within TSFM backbones: Moirai-MoE~\citep{liu2025moiraimoe} routes frequency bands across internal experts, and Time-MoE~\citep{shi2024timemoe} scales to billions of parameters via sparse activation. These apply MoE \emph{within} the backbone architecture; segment-wise approaches further specialize experts by temporal segments. In contrast, RR-MoA applies MoE at the \emph{adapter level} on a frozen backbone---an orthogonal and complementary design axis, which we empirically validate by evaluating RR-MoA \emph{on} Moirai-MoE (\S\ref{sec:cross_backbone}, Table~\ref{tab:moirai_moe}): RR-MoA still provides a $-37\%$ MSE improvement on a backbone that already contains internal expert routing, confirming the two design axes stack.
83	
84	\textbf{MoE routing collapse.} A large body of work addresses routing collapse via \emph{optimization-side} interventions: Switch Transformer load-balance~\citep{fedus2022switch}, ST-MoE z-loss~\citep{zoph2022stmoe}, Expert-Choice routing~\citep{zhou2022expertchoice}, and ReMoE ReLU gates~\citep{zhang2025remoe}. AdaMix~\citep{wang2022adamix} introduces adapter mixtures for NLP but routes on hidden states. All enforce diversity via optimization constraints without addressing whether the router's input contains discriminative information. We sweep eleven such mechanisms on MOMENT$+$RevIN and show \textbf{none recover MSE to within $2.7{\times}$ of RR-MoA} (Table~\ref{tab:rescue}). Our contribution is complementary: we identify when routing information is structurally absent from hidden states, and propose moving the router's input out of the normalized representation as the minimal causal fix.
85	
86	\textbf{Normalization in Time Series.} RevIN~\citep{kim2021revin} mitigates distribution shift; DishTS~\citep{fan2023dishts} and Non-stationary Transformers~\citep{liu2022nonstationary} partially recover stripped statistics---both recognizing that instance normalization discards predictive information. We identify a previously unknown consequence: RevIN destroys the per-window statistics that MoE routers require for discriminative gating. RR-MoA operationalizes the ``statistic re-injection'' insight specifically for routing.
87	
88	\textbf{NAS and LLM-Guided Search.} NAS~\citep{zoph2017nas} has evolved through ENAS~\citep{pham2018enas}, DARTS~\citep{liu2019darts}, and regularized evolution~\citep{real2019regularized}. Shears~\citep{munoz2024shears} and RZ-NAS~\citep{ji2025rznas} search adapter hyperparameters for LLMs; NAS-LoRA~\citep{chen2025naslora} targets vision adapters. FunSearch~\citep{romera2024funsearch}, EvoPrompting~\citep{chen2023evoprompting}, PartEvo~\citep{hu2025partevo}, and ReEvo~\citep{ye2024reevo} demonstrate LLM-guided program synthesis. We use this paradigm for expert pool construction (Appendix~\ref{app:aas_details}).
89	
90	\section{Method}
91	\label{sec:method}
```

1196	\textbf{What the router learns.} Per-sample routing decisions correlate with amplitude and volatility---the very statistics RevIN strips (Figure~\ref{fig:routing_viz}). On ETTh1, Expert~0 (mean-pool) serves as the generalist ($53\%$), while Expert~2 (max-pool) handles low-amplitude windows and Expert~3 (attention-pool) handles high-amplitude windows. The router reserves specialized processing for windows at the distribution tails, empirically validating the conditional independence assumption $E \perp\!\!\!\perp S \mid (M,\Sigma)$ of Part~(iii) of Proposition~\ref{thm:mi_decomp}.

  "new_string": "Our contribution is complementary: we identify when routing information is structurally absent from hidden states, and propose moving the router's input out of the normalized representation as the minimal causal fix. Concurrently, recent work eliminates external routers entirely: AoE~\\citep{lv2025aoe} lets experts self-select via activation norms, ERMoE~\\citep{cheng2025ermoe} routes via cosine similarity to learned eigenbases (achieving 0\\% collapse without balancing losses), and Routing-Free MoE~\\citep{liu2026routingfree} encapsulates gating within each expert. We validate this self-routing principle in our setting (\\S\\ref{sec:main_results}): self-gated experts on raw input match RR-MoA, while the same architecture on hidden states collapses---confirming the bottleneck is normalization, not router design."
}

{

  "old_string": "\\textbf{Architecture-agnostic validation.} Replacing AdaMix's hidden-state router with a raw-input router recovers entropy to $1.41$--$1.57$ and MSE by $41$--$88\\%$ on all 12 cells---the fix generalizes beyond RR-MoA (Appendix~\\ref{app:adamix_details}).",
  "new_string": "\\textbf{Self-routing validation.} To confirm the bottleneck is in the routing \\emph{input} rather than the routing \\emph{architecture}, we test Self-Routed MoA (SR-MoA): each expert contains its own sigmoid gate on the raw input---no external router, no softmax, no load-balancing loss~\\citep{lv2025aoe,cheng2025ermoe,liu2026routingfree}. On raw input, SR-MoA achieves MSE competitive with RR-MoA (ETTh1: $0.468 \\pm 0.010$; ETTm1: $0.389 \\pm 0.019$; Weather: $0.221 \\pm 0.016$; 5 seeds, frozen). The same architecture gating on \\emph{hidden states} collapses: MSE degrades $2.0$--$2.3\\times$ (ETTh1: $1.031$; ETTm1: $0.923$; Weather: $0.509$) with entropy ${\\to}\\log K$ (near-uniform, no routing). This is a fourth independent causal control---alongside the rescue sweep, AdaMix-Raw, and Timer-XL opposite trajectory---all confirming the mechanism diagnosed by Proposition~\\ref{thm:mi_decomp}.\n\n\\textbf{Architecture-agnostic validation.} Replacing AdaMix's hidden-state router with a raw-input router recovers entropy to $1.41$--$1.57$ and MSE by $41$--$88\\%$ on all 12 cells---the fix generalizes beyond RR-MoA (Appendix~\\ref{app:adamix_details})."
}

1213	\textbf{What the router learns.} Per-sample routing decisions correlate with amplitude and volatility---the very statistics RevIN strips (Figure~\ref{fig:routing_viz}). On ETTh1, Expert~0 (mean-pool) serves as the generalist ($53\%$), while Expert~2 (max-pool) handles low-amplitude windows and Expert~3 (attention-pool) handles high-amplitude windows. The router reserves specialized processing for windows at the distribution tails, empirically validating the conditional independence assumption $E \perp\!\!\!\perp S \mid (M,\Sigma)$ of Part~(iii) of Proposition~\ref{thm:mi_decomp}.
1214

Colors indicate dominant expert (top-1 routing weight). Expert selection clusters by amplitude and volatility---the very statistics RevIN strips---empirically validating the conditional independence assumption $(M,\Sigma) \perp\!\!\!\perp S$ from Proposition~\ref{thm:mi_decomp}(iii).}

> AGENT

Here's my analysis and the experiments I'm launching. The three remaining vulnerabilities in priority order:

**Vulnerability 1 — "DLinear gap" (biggest remaining weakness):** DLinear still beats frozen MOMENT+RR-MoA on some cells. SR-MoA is frozen-only so far.

**Vulnerability 2 — "Shallow coverage of SR-MoA":** Only H=96, only frozen, only MOMENT-small. A reviewer will say "you tested this on one config."

**Vulnerability 3 — "Frozen paradox generality":** Does the frozen > unfrozen paradox hold for SR-MoA too? If yes, this is a second independent confirmation of the paradox.

I'll launch 4 experiment batches in parallel:
